Role: **Reviewer** (agent `native-render`). Write scope: this file only. Never source, never runtime.
Owner of the gate task: `task-8` (already in_progress/owned; `team_task_update claim` returned
"not ready to claim" — tracked, not blocking).

Sources of truth used for every verdict:
- `D:\hake\lotus\PORT_BRIEF.md` (offsets/build/launch), `D:\hake\lotus\TEAM_PROTOCOL.md` (roles/quality bar).
- Reference scripts (read-only): `C:\Users\USER\AppData\Roaming\xYjGsPeqoWTEzbif\scripts\Self Play.lua` +
  `...\scripts\self_play\**` (the 16.11 original this port must match).
- Current source files under `D:\hake\lotus\src\` (read in full before judging; there is no git, so I reason
  about the current file content, not diffs).
- Runtime evidence: `D:\hake\lotus\host.log` (grepped, not trusted blindly) and offline execution
  (`D:\hake\lotus\luajit\src\src\luajit.exe`) where a module can run without Dota.
- Metrics probes already compiled/run in this workspace (`src\_probe_font.cpp`, `src\_probe_width.cpp`) when
  native font claims need numbers.

## Review method (applied to every declared-ready slice)

1. Read the full current file(s) in the write scope; cite `file:line` for every finding.
2. Cross-check semantics against the actual reference call sites (not against the task description). A task
   description claim ("scripts use it", "verified") is not evidence — the reference call site or a log line is.
3. Check LuaJIT 5.1 vs Lua 5.4 gotchas: integer/float formatting, `math.type`, `utf8`, `table.unpack`,
   `#` on sparse tables, integer division, `pcall` NOT catching native access violations.
4. Check nil/type contracts at every boundary the reference crosses (allocation-free vs nil returns, table vs
   number for bitmask helpers, `pcall` swallowing programming errors).
5. Check hot-path cost: per-tick budget target **<300 ms**; no full entity scans per event callback; no
   per-call large FFI allocations; no per-order/per-frame file I/O on the tick thread.
6. Check crash/reentrancy/threading: Lua state is single-threaded (`HostThread`), D3D Present hook and order
   drain run on the game/render thread; anything calling engine natives from `HostThread` is a data race.
7. Check state leakage between matches/reloads and single-loop safety (no second loop, stale caches keyed by
   entity index must be invalidated).
8. Claim-vs-code honesty: every "verified/implemented" claim must follow from the code or have evidence.
9. Verdict: **PASS** or **REJECT**. REJECT lists exact required fixes. QA evidence is only final closure after
   a PASS here.
10. Append entry with timestamp + owner + files; message owner + `pm`. Re-review after fixes (new entry).
11. **Hard evidence rule (PM 20:12): the QA test hero is ALWAYS Treant Protector
    (`npc_dota_hero_treant`) in a single-player bot game.** Any live PASS claim whose evidence is not
    hero=Treant is automatically REJECT (invalid evidence; ask QA to remake). Offline unit tests and Lua
    syntax checks are fine for code-shape review but never substitute for a Treant live run.

### W4 — `src/render.lua`, `src/overlay.lua`, `src/menu.lua` (owner `developer`; QA `ui-hud`)
- [ ] `Render.TextSize(...)` returns exactly the width the native renderer advances (same font/size/weight),

- [ ] Input latency <1 frame while menu open even when the bot tick is idle (pump cadence is not only driven by

- [ ] HUD refresh cadence: text/boxes are republished per bot tick (1-5 s in match) — confirm what the task
      claims ("per-frame HUD") and state the real cadence; world markers only are per-frame.
- [ ] `PushClip/PopClip`: reference 16.11 scripts contain **0** calls (grep) — priority downgraded; if
      implemented, protocol change mirrored on both sides.
- [ ] No allocation per frame in the publish path, no unbounded command growth (publish caps 4096/8192/1024).

### W5 — `src/render11.cpp` + `src/host.cpp` (owner `developer`; QA relaunch required)
- [ ] RCmd ABI: `int kind; float x,y,z,w; unsigned color; int size; char text[64]; float x2,y2,z2;` = 104 bytes,
      offset-of text=28, x2=92 — Lua cdef must match exactly (static check).
- [ ] **Vertex buffer capacity vs text length**: render11.cpp:534 sizes the buffer `(hudN+menuN)*64+4096`
      verts, but one 63-char text command emits up to 63*6=378 verts → out-of-bounds `PushVert` (heap
      corruption/crash) for long tooltips/labels. Need exact pre-pass sizing or per-command growth; prove with a
      stress publish.
- [ ] World-marker projection matrix: `ProjectW` uses `g_viewPrev` (render11.cpp:283-298) while `g_viewCur` is
      the matrix just read at Present → one-frame-stale projection = jitter while the camera moves; confirm the
      intended matrix with an in-match A/B before claiming no jitter. host.cpp's legacy GDI layered-window
      marker thread (host.cpp:391-492, 33 ms) draws the same line again if ever started — verify it is dead
      (no caller) or remove.
- [ ] Present state save/restore: scissor rects are never saved/restored while rasterizer state carries
      `ScissorEnable`; a game scissor left enabled by the game at Present would clip the overlay (and restoring
      the old RS state without rect restore can leak our clip into the game). Add RSGetScissorRects/RSSet.
- [ ] Per-frame allocations: `CreateRenderTargetView` + `GetBuffer`/`Release` every Present (render11.cpp:504-514)
      contradict "never allocate per frame"; cache RTV keyed by swapchain buffer.
- [ ] Menu open state split: host.cpp:315 `g_menuOpen` vs render11.cpp:403 `R11_SetMenuOpen`
      (overlay.lua:374 closes via `R11_SetMenuOpen(0)` only) → after Esc-close, the next Del toggles the host
      flag to 0 and the menu needs two Del presses. Unify to one flag.
- [ ] Input safety: `UiKeyHook` swallows Del globally even when another app has focus (host.cpp:354-363); if the
      Lua state dies while the menu is open, every key is swallowed forever (no liveness timeout). Both must be
      fixed and tested.
- [ ] Order queue drain (host.cpp:156-179): `orderIssuer` hardcoded 3, `PtrOk` guards, `g_fwdQueue` forwarding
      to host6 must not double-execute; currently unused because orders.lua disables the queue (see O1).
- [ ] Font metrics: Bahnschrift `tmAscent==px`, `tmHeight=1.2*px`, current atlas cell `px+6` exactly fits
      (probe output _probe_font); drawing 28px atlas scaled to 9-16px with LINEAR gives soft text and
      `TextSize` width from 28px advances ≠ GDI width at requested size (right-aligned HUD drifts). Needs
      per-size rasterization or a documented ≤1 px tolerance proven on the HUD.
- [ ] Overlay cost measured (extend R11 frame log with draw ms + vert count), <1 ms; no Present stall; no
      per-frame command copy bigger than needed (static arrays ok).
- [ ] Crash safety: Present hook prologue decode (host.cpp:236-279) must skip (not corrupt) on unknown prologue;
      no our-RTV creation that breaks flip-model (current path uses current backbuffer — keep).

## Gate log

### 2026-11-16 20:16 — W3 (orders.lua + nav.lua + GridNav wiring) — owner `orders-nav` — **REJECT**

Files read: `src/nav.lua` (376 lines), `src/orders.lua` (127 lines), `src/loader.lua:129-181` (GridNav wiring).
No item was formally declared ready by its owner; this is the Lead-directed first target. **QA must not record
closure for W3.** Offline test `src/test_nav.lua` does pass (`15 checks, 0 failed`), but it only exercises
`setObstacles()`/A*, not `rescan`, `refresh`, GridNav wiring, npc-map, or any live data.

Blocking findings (required fixes):

1. **N1 — refresh uses two different clocks → nav becomes permanently stale mid-match.**
   `nav.lua:147-149` stores `lastRefresh` from `GameRules.GetGameTime()` (match clock, seconds, `-90` in the
   lobby) while `nav.lua:153,157-162` compares against `os.clock()` (process **CPU** time). Concrete
   consequences: lobby/pre-game (`host.log` shows `gt=-90`) → `os.clock()-(-90) > 2` every tick ⇒ full rescan
   every tick (67 600-cell clear + 67 600-iteration edge loop + entity scan, see N4); once match time exceeds
   CPU time (a few minutes in) the difference goes negative ⇒ **no rescan for the rest of the match** ⇒ trees
   destroyed/buildings fallen are never reflected. Required: one wall clock for both store and compare
   (e.g. `ffi.C.GetTickCount64`), and log `nav: rescan ms=… obstacles=…` at most once/10 s.

2. **N2 — silent failure paths (mask the old bugs).** `nav.lua:120-135` wraps the whole entity scan in `pcall`
   and `return 0` without any log; `clearGrid()` (`nav.lua:116`) already wiped the previous grid, so any error
   leaves `built=true` with an empty grid ⇒ everything traversable except the map edge, i.e. the exact
   "walks into trees" bug returns invisibly. Same class: `loader.lua:144-147` (nav error → bounds-only `true`)
   and `loader.lua:163-179` (nav path failure → silent straight-line fallback). Required: log-once per failure
   class + counter, build into a scratch mask and swap only on success.

3. **N3 — hot-path allocation/perf, no fast path.** Each `nav.buildPath` allocates 3 FFI arrays over
   `W*H = 260*260 = 67 600` cells (`nav.lua:260-267`: ~270 KB float + 270 KB int32 + 67 KB uint8) and fills
   them with a 135 k-iteration Lua loop, plus fresh `heap`/`keys` tables (`nav.lua:270-271`). Measured offline
   (verbatim): `perf: 20k isTraversable=1.0ms (0.05us/call) longPath=11.0ms n=12 midPath=1.60ms`. Reference
   callers can request up to 12 paths per `navigation.escape` (`navigation.lua:107`) and ~24 candidates × 2
   `safePath` calls per `navigation.detour` (`navigation.lua:217,220`) ⇒ worst case tens of A* runs per tick,
   ～50–500 ms+, breaching the <300 ms tick budget on the same thread that pumps HUD/input. Required: persistent
   scratch buffers (memset, not Lua fill), straight-line LOS fast path (return single endpoint), expiring path
   cache keyed (startCell,destCell,ignoreTrees), expansion budget that returns a partial path.

4. **N4 — `CreateNpcMap`/4th `map` arg silently ignored ⇒ creep-block pathing is a no-op while the script thinks
   it is active.** `loader.lua:157` returns `{}` (truthy), `loader.lua:164` calls
   `navmod.buildPath(sx,sy,dx,dy,ignoreTrees==true)` — the reference's npc-map argument is dropped and
   `nav.lua` has no npc support at all. Reference relies on it: `navigation.lua:268-300` (creep blocking),
   `last_hit.lua:493-494,552-554,567,599`. Required: return `nil` from `CreateNpcMap` so
   `navigation.lua:298` cleanly disables the feature (honest "not implemented"), or implement temporary blocked
   discs from the excluded/blocker units; never fake success.

5. **N5 — obstacle source and blocking radius are unverified.** `nav.lua:105-113` classifies only by
   designer-name substring (tree 34, tower/barracks 150, fort 260, fountain 420, shop 180) and `nav.lua:69-87`
   marks whole 64 px cells whose *centre* is within the radius. There is no repo evidence that live tree
   entities exist in the client entity list: the only log hit for `ent_dota_tree` is a class-name list line
   (`host.log 04:21:03`, between `ent_dota_shop` and `info_player_start_badguys`), not a spawn dump. If trees
   are absent the whole tree mask is a silent no-op; if present, a 34 px disc on a 64 px grid closes forest
   corridors, and per-cell centre tests cannot guarantee hull clearance (a point up to ~45 px from a blocked
   centre still reads walkable). Required: live diag counting marked cells per class + path success/straight
   ratio in a real match before any PASS.

6. **N6 — expansion cap returns `{}` (no path) → loader falls back to straight line.** `nav.lua:234`
   `maxExpand=24000`, `nav.lua:311 if not found then return {}`. Dense forest/buildings or an unreachable
   destination silently yields empty path → `loader.lua:171-179` straight line. Required: best-effort partial
   path (best-h cell reached) or explicit nil + log; callers must distinguish.

7. **O1 — BLOCKER crash risk: unit orders are issued from the wrong thread.** `orders.lua:27-33` hardcodes
   `queueAvailable()=false` ("hooking DXGI Present black-screens the game on this build") so
   `orders.lua:110` calls `PrepareUnitOrders` (client.dll+0x1B5F860) **directly from the Lua `HostThread`**.
   `host.cpp:107-110` documents precisely why that is unsafe ("races the game thread and can crash Dota") and
   `host.cpp:156-179` already implements the safe Present-thread drain. The stated justification is contradicted
   by current evidence: the Present hook is installed and drawing every frame (`host.log` `R11: frame …` lines;
   `host.cpp:280` "Present hook installed"). Also `hostlib` (orders.lua:26) is never assigned, so the queue
   branch (orders.lua:104) would nil-index even if enabled (pcall hides it). `pcall` cannot catch an access
   violation, so a race here is a hard crash, not a Lua error. Required: assign `hostlib`, enable the queue when
   `QueueOrder` exists, keep direct call only as a loud fallback; QA proves orders still execute (`[order] PUO
   type=` lines + hero moves) and Dota survives >10 min of ordering.

8. **O2 — per-order full entity scans.** `orders.lua:90-92` calls `localController()` (`orders.lua:60-70`, entity
   iteration) and `localHero()` (`orders.lua:72-76` → `entity.getLocalHero()` iteration) on **every** order;
   api.lua:556 `Players.GetLocal()` iterates too. High order rates (last-hit/deny/attack) multiply scans on the
   tick thread. Required: cache controller/hero/hero-instance per tick, invalidate on hero/match change; measure
   orders/tick and scan ms in a live log.

9. **N7 — non-ffi fallback is broken (latent):** `nav.lua:261-266` leaves `gScore={}` uninitialised and
   `nav.lua:299` compares `ng < gScore[ni]` (number vs nil ⇒ error) if `ffi` is unavailable, although the module
   advertises the fallback. Either delete the fallback branches or initialise them. Minor dead code:
   `nav.lua:28` (`CELL` never assigned), `nav.lua:53-62` (`markCell` unused), `nav.lua:347-356` (`advanced` is
   always true).

10. **N8 — z coordinate not documented:** path points get `z=0` (`nav.lua:325`) and the loader substitutes
    `dest.z` for **all** waypoints (`loader.lua:167`); multi-segment paths lose per-point ground z. Acceptable as
    an approximation only if documented in code; risky for `CAST_POSITION` on a waypoint.

Passed parts (keep): path interface contract matches the reference consumers — excludes start, last point
snapped to destination (`nav.lua:358-359` vs `navigation.lua:49-67`); `ent.iter()`/`designerName`/`GetAbsOrigin`
call shapes are correct against `entity.lua:96` and `api.lua:131`; 8-neighbour A* corner-cutting check is sound;
diagonal heuristic is admissible.

### 2026-11-16 20:16 — W2 subset (16.11 API shims in loader.lua:212-360) — owner `loader-events` — **REJECT**

Files read: `src/loader.lua` (full), plus consumer code in the reference scripts and `api.lua`, `schema.lua`,
`entity.lua`, `enums.lua`. No declared-ready message received; Lead-directed first target. **QA must not record
closure for these shims.** What is correct: `table.unpack/pack`, `math.type/tointeger` (loader.lua:213-222);
`utf8.len` matches the only reference use (`localization.lua:13` `utf8.len(value) or #value`); `Entity.GetAbsOrigin`
returns a single `mathx.Vector` (`api.lua:131`) so the `local x,y,z` destructuring pattern is fine;
`Abilities.AttemptToUpgrade` ends up passing an entity through `orderIndex` (`api.lua:496-506`) — the unused
`idx` at loader.lua:308 is dead but harmless.

Blocking findings (required fixes):

1. **L1 — `FogOfWar.IsPointVisible` hardcoded `true` (loader.lua:342) actively inverts reference logic.** Six
   reference call sites depend on both outcomes: `awareness.lua:151,165` (fog spots as hunt candidates),
   `detection.lua:90` (ward/detection placement), `jungle.lua:425`, `runes.lua:150` (closes rune spots as
   "spawn is visible and empty"), `support_camps.lua:244`. Always-true is not a neutral stub. Required: a real
   visibility check (or `false` + log-once + known-gap marker) and a live diag proving both true/false occur.

2. **L2 — `Modifier.GetState` returns `{}` (loader.lua:301) — wrong type, breaks the consumer.** Reference
   `invoker.lua:95-96` feeds it to `planner.hasFlag` (`planner.lua:11`: `math.floor(mask / flag) % 2 == 1`) ⇒
   "attempt to perform arithmetic on a table value" whenever an enemy is stunned and Invoker evaluates
   `stunnedFor`, i.e. Invoker stun-duration estimation errors out; the loader tick pcall logs an error **every
   tick** (loader.lua:444). Required: return the numeric modifier-state bitmask (find the real
   `CDOTA_Buff` state field) — never a table; verify the Invoker path live.

3. **L3 — Modifier timing helpers silently default (`loader.lua:273-289`).** `GetDieTime/GetDuration/GetStackCount/
   GetCreationTime` return `0/1` when `schema.offsetOf("CDOTA_Buff", …)` returns nil, and `schema.lua:84` returns
   nil silently. `common.remaining` (`heroes/common.lua:51`) = `GetDieTime - now` ⇒ 0 ⇒ every buff looks
   expired; 9 reference call sites use `Modifier.GetDieTime`. Required: live probe logging the resolved offsets
   and a sample buff's dieTime; log-once if an offset is missing.

4. **L4 — `NetChannel.GetAvgLatency` (loader.lua:343-346) ignores the flow enum and uses an unverified offset.**
   Reference has 20 call sites using `Enum.Flow.FLOW_OUTGOING`/`FLOW_INCOMING` separately (`last_hit.lua:50,626`,
   `creep_block.lua:7`, `aim.lua:14,60`, …). PORT_BRIEF has **no** latency offset; `ctrl+0x948` is not backed by

   latency while no log says so. Required: verify the offset live (compare against real ping, outgoing vs
   incoming), or document the identical-value approximation and log it.

5. **L5 — `GameRules.IsPaused` = `GetGameState()==6` (loader.lua:339) is the POST_GAME state, not pause.**
   `enums.lua:613-614`: GAME_IN_PROGRESS=5, POST_GAME=6 ⇒ a real pause (state stays 5) is never detected, and

6. **L6 — `Tower.GetAttackTarget` offset unverified (loader.lua:347-350, t+0x1B5C).** 8 tower-aggro call sites

7. **L7 — `GlobalVars.GetAbsFrameTime` returns the match clock (loader.lua:335-338).** Only used by

8. **L8 — `utf8.char` is wrong (loader.lua:240): `string.char(cp)` errors for cp>255 instead of UTF-8

9. **L9 — `Abilities.AttemptToUpgrade` is unreachable via its only reference caller** (`talents.lua:31-38` goes

## Items flagged ahead of a formal review (no verdict yet; owners should not read these as PASS)

- **W5 native crash risk (developer, task-3):** `render11.cpp:534` vertex capacity `(hudN+menuN)*64+4096` is
  smaller than a single 63-char text (378 verts) ⇒ possible OOB write on long labels/tooltips. Also
  `g_viewPrev` one-frame-stale projection (render11.cpp:283-298), missing scissor-rect save/restore at Present,
  RTV allocated per frame, and the split menu-open flag `host.cpp:315` vs `render11.cpp:403` (Esc-close leaves

`overlay.lua` 20:14:55); `loadfile` 3/3 OK (re-verified). QA must not treat this slice as closed.

  (Self Play.lua:88) → `:Create(page)` nesting → groups with `Enum.GroupSide.Left/Right` (Self Play.lua:89-170,
  draft.lua:39-108); `Group:Type()==Enum.WidgetType.MenuGroup(4)` required by
  `localization.group` (localization.lua:122-125) and it returns 4 (menu.lua Group:Type). `Widget:Get(name)`,
  `List()`, `ListEnabled()` (matchmaking.lua:37), `Update(options, selected)` rebuilding a multi map from each
  option's 3rd field (draft.lua:96 vs menu.lua Update), `SetCallback(function(widget))` (draft.lua:79),
  `DragAllowed`, `ForceLocalization`, `ToolTip`, `Visible` are all implemented and match consumer shapes.
  `localization.apply` (localization.lua:31-40) exercising `Update(options, selected)` + `Set(selected)` works.
- `PushClip/PopClip` (render.lua:280-295) emit kinds 10/11; native ignores unknown kinds safely
  (`render11.cpp:357-367` → `DrawWorldCmd` handles only 7/8/9). No 16.11 reference script calls them (grep 0)
  → no visual regression today. Protocol for the future native work: kind 10 = x,y,z,w = x0,y0,x1,y1; kind 11
  = pop.
- `Circle` arc branch is unreachable from the 16.11 scripts (only `hud.lua:324` calls `Circle` with 4 args) →
  low risk. Per-present cost (`hudFromFrame` Lua table + `ffi.new("RCmd[?]")`, overlay.lua:480-532) is
  acceptable at the real cadence (once per bot tick 1–5 s in-match); pooling is an optional optimisation, not a
  blocker, unless QA shows `loader.drawOnly` running at frame rate.
- P0-B root cause is genuinely removed at the menu layer: catch-all now typed + logged
  (`menu.lua:33-59`), `IsDown` is kind-gated to bind widgets and `pcall`-guarded (`menu.lua:149-157`).
  For the sweep question: the only boolean-intent widget query in all 136 reference scripts is
  `ui.pause:IsDown()` (Self Play.lua:1860); `IsPressed` has 0 call sites. `ffi` and the LuaJIT `bit` global are
  both available in this host (verified: `bit` exists, `ffi.cdef GetAsyncKeyState` at menu.lua:6-7).

Blocking findings (REJECT):

1. **R1 — bind keys are in the wrong domain: Valve `ButtonCode` vs Windows `VK` (P0-B is fixed only
   partially).** `Widget:IsDown` (menu.lua:149-157) calls `GetAsyncKeyState(widget.value)` directly, but the
   script's default bind value is a Valve ButtonCode: `general:Bind("Pause while held",
   Enum.ButtonCode.KEY_SPACE)` (Self Play.lua:93) and `enums.lua:741` defines `KEY_SPACE = 65`, while Windows
   `VK_SPACE = 0x20`. `GetAsyncKeyState(65)` tests **VK 0x41 = the A key**. Consequences: holding Space does not
   pause; holding **A (attack)** pauses the bot for as long as it is held → the "Paused: manual control" symptom
   can still reproduce during normal play. The mismatch is bidirectional: overlay bind capture stores a raw
   Windows VK (`overlay.lua:696 bindCapture:Set(vk)` where `vk` is native `KBDLLHOOKSTRUCT.vkCode`), yet
   `keyName` (overlay.lua:139-150) and IsDown interpret the same number as different domains (a bind captured as
   Space would display as "A"). Required fix:
   (a) full Valve ButtonCode → Windows VK table in `Widget:IsDown` (KEY_0..9 1..10→0x30..0x39;
   KEY_A..Z 11..36→0x41..0x5A; KEY_PAD_0..9 37..46→0x60..0x69; KEY_ENTER 64→0x0D, KEY_SPACE 65→0x20,
   BACKSPACE 66→0x08, TAB 67→0x09, ESC 70→0x1B, INSERT/DELETE/HOME/END/PAGE 72..77→0x2D..0x22, SHIFT/ALT/CTRL
   79..84→0xA0..0xA3, UP/LEFT/DOWN/RIGHT 88..91→0x26/0x25/0x28/0x27, F1..F12 92..→0x70..0x7B; punctuation
   53..63→0xDB/0xDD/0xBA/0xDE/0xC0/0xBC/0xBE/0xBF/0xDC/0xBD/0xBB; pad 47..52→0x6F/0x6A/0x6D/0x6B/0x0D/0x6E);
   (b) keep the widget value in **ButtonCode** domain (Umbrella parity) and convert captured WinVK→ButtonCode in
   overlay before `Set`; (c) `keyName` takes ButtonCode and renders from the same table; (d) add an offline
   assert test (KEY_SPACE→VK 0x20; KEY_A→0x41) — no live run needed for the mapping itself.
   Note the cross-dependency the developer flagged: pause expiry also needs a monotonically advancing
   `GameRules.GetGameTime` (api-core P0-A); a frozen clock plus one false arm = permanent pause regardless of
   this fix.
2. **R2 — per-widget `Visible(false)` is ignored by the ClickGUI renderer/hit tests.** `groupHeight`
   (overlay.lua:193-198) and `drawGroup` (overlay.lua:283-296) iterate `g.widgets` without checking
   `w.visible ~= false`; only groups are filtered (overlay.lua:199-204). The reference hides widgets:
   `draft.lua:97 widget:Visible(role == ui.editRole:Get() + 1)` keeps only one of the five MultiSelect priority
   lists visible (the other four must disappear and release row height). Today all five render, are
   height-counted and remain clickable. Required: filter `w.visible ~= false` in both places.
3. **R3 — unknown-method logging covers methods but not callback errors; `mlog` is still a hard no-op**
   (menu.lua:9; `fire()` at menu.lua:74-78 swallows callback exceptions silently). A silently failing
   `SetCallback` changes bot behaviour with zero evidence. Required: route `mlog` (or at least
   callback-error paths) to `host.log` once per key. Not UI-blocking but it is claim-vs-evidence.
4. **R4 — minor:** `openDropdown` calls `menuSpace()` twice per open (`select(1, menuSpace())`,
   `select(2, menuSpace())`, overlay.lua:628-629) — cache the pair (2× R11_Size + ffi allocs).
5. **Known gap, decision needed (not blocking this slice):** `localization.init()` looks up
   `Menu.Find("SettingsHidden","","","","Main","Language")` (localization.lua:107) — our tree has no
   `SettingsHidden` root, so it returns early and the UI language is permanently "en". Adding a hidden root
   requires overlay to exclude hidden roots from the root tab row (`maybeRow("root", menu.tree)`,
   overlay.lua:410-413) or it will render as a user-visible tab. Either implement both sides or record the gap.

   Structural fix: expose api.lua's MBI type/`VirtualQuery` wrapper as a shared helper instead of two
   independent declarations.
2. **L2 — `OnPrepareUnitOrders` payload consumers: shape is right, but the human/bot discrimination cannot be
   signed off yet.** `core.manual` (Self Play.lua:2164-2172) requires `data.player == ctx.player`,
   `data.identifier == nil`, and `data.npc == ctx.hero` (with `orderIssuer == SELECTED_UNITS`) — loader's payload
   (loader.lua:927-932) satisfies that. The seq bookkeeping is wired (loader.lua:986-1000 consumes
   `orders.lastSeq` before/after each prepare; `ownSeq` exposed at loader.lua:986, `orders.lua:458` updates
   `lastSeq`), and reset on seq jump (loader.lua:879-884). Remaining risks to prove: any engine-internal seq
   increment not produced by `orders.prepare` counts as "human" (the code logs a WARNING at loader.lua:922-926 —
   good), and payload `npc=local hero` for any order that did not control the hero is an over-pause
   approximation (documented). Required: live log showing (a) `[pause] human order armed` only on real human
   clicks, (b) **no** `[pause] RE-ARMED before expiry` from bot-only play, (c) resume log after 4 s with a
   moving game clock.
3. **L3 — projectile payload vs consumers (static check).** Consumers use
   `source,target,moveSpeed,isAttack,handle,ability` (Self Play.lua:2193-2218; `data.ability` may be nil,
   `data.handle` must be unique per projectile since it is a table key at Self Play.lua:2217). Loader's
   attack-projectile scheduling must therefore set a fresh unique `handle`; `expireTime/maxImpactTime/
   original_move_speed` are unused by these consumers but are part of the documented payload — diag_events must
   dump exactly the 18 keys so QA can diff. No PASS until diag output is attached.
4. **L4 — coverage honesty:** linear projectiles / real particles / USERMSG-driven events are declared GAP by
   the owner (loader.lua:392-394) — that is honest and acceptable as a known gap, but the task-2 claim
   "callback coverage matrix" must not be reported as complete; OnParticle*/OnLinearProjectile* call sites in
   the reference will never fire. Record in PM_STATUS/PLAN, not as PASS.
5. **L5 (sweep, architect request) — `loader.lua:54-64 stubNS` is the remaining untyped catch-all.**
   Unlike api.lua's `softIndex` (api.lua:1689-1704: Is/Can/Has/Should→false, Duration/Modifier-getters→{},
   Get*→0, logs `API missing:` once) and menu.lua's new typed fallback, `stubNS` still installs
   `function() return nil end` for **any** unknown member of namespaces api.lua does not own
   (Abilities/Couriers/Camps/Runes/TempTrees/Towers/Trees/PhysicalItems/Modifiers/LinearProjectiles/Input/
   World/FogOfWar/GlobalVars/NetChannel/GC/...). Consequences: `if NS.X then` capability checks are always
   true; `Is*` calls return nil (falsy but nil, so `a and b or c` patterns change behaviour); arithmetic on a
   nil return throws; and nothing is logged. Required: mirror menu/api policy — typed fallback
   (Is*/Can*/Has*/Should*→false, List*/GetAll*→{}, numeric getters→0, else nil) + log-once per name.
   `api.lua softIndex` has one residual risk to include in the sweep: **`Get*`→0 breaks table-returning
   getters** (e.g. a missing `GetAll/GetModifiers` used in `ipairs(GetX())` throws "table expected, got
   number") and `Hero.GetCurrentXP` is currently a missing getter returning 0 (probe evidence) so
   value.lua:47 always sees 0 XP — acceptable only with a logged known-gap because it silently changes
   level/XP decisions.

### 2026-11-16 20:37 — task-13 (P2.1 HUD Lua: TextSize exactness, HUD overlap guard, marker ordering) — owner `developer` — **PASS with one required evidence fix**

Files read: `render.lua` 20:23:14 (TextSize 142-168), `overlay.lua` 20:24:34 (hudFromFrame 502-564, dumpHud 566-584, hudLog 607-623, present 625-642), `dev_ui_check.lua` 20:24:34. Offline: `luajit dev_ui_check.lua` → `dev_ui_check: 20 checks, 0 failed` (re-run by me).

Verified-good:
- **TextSize exactness is real**: `overlay.textWidth` now returns `(w, true)` from `R11_TextWidthW` and the estimate `(#t*s*0.5, false)` only when the host is unavailable (overlay.lua:63-71); `Render.TextSize` returns that `w` with `Vec2(w,size)`, empty string → `(0,size)` without warning (render.lua:153-168), and the fallback is logged once (`[ui] textwidth-fallback`). The native width function advances by the same `adv*scale` the renderer draws with (render11.cpp:370-387), so right/center alignment from hud.lua is internally exact — the remaining softness at small sizes is the parked native atlas item (task-3), not a TextSize defect.
- **Order preservation**: `hudFromFrame` appends commands in `ipairs(frame)` order, world kinds 7/8/9 included (overlay.lua:510-544) → Lua publish order == native draw order; no re-sorting found.
- **Overlap guard**: text boxes sized with the exact native widths + `h=ceil(size*1.2)` (matches Bahnschrift ink height 1.2*em), excludes the deliberate 1px shadow pair via same-text/≤2px test (matches hud.lua:37-38 `(x+1,y+1)`), counts real >2px intersections and off-screen text (overlay.lua:548-562); `hud_cmds.txt` header carries the same stats (572-573); stats survive the call `pcall(hudLog,...)` (633).
- Off-screen/behind-camera handling matches the reference contract: Lua `WorldToScreen` already rejects `w<0.001`/`|ndc|>1.35` (render.lua:103-131) so hud.lua's `if not visible` guards never publish those markers; native re-checks `ProjectW` — refusing to invent edge markers is the correct parity choice.
- Existing host.log has no `[hud]`/`[ui] textwidth-fallback` lines because the running Lua state predates the 20:23/20:24 files — QA reload required (expected).

**Required fix before QA closure (evidence mechanism is wrong as written):** `hudLog` counts `present()` calls and logs every 300 of them, with the comment "300 presents at 60 fps ≈ 5 s" (overlay.lua:607-616). But `overlay.present` is called once per **bot tick** (loader.tick → present; in-match ticks were 1.3-1.9 s in the live log), not once per rendered frame — so the clean-run `[hud]` line appears every ~7-10 minutes, and QA's acceptance grep (`Select-String host.log -Pattern '\[hud\]' | Select -Last 5`) will usually return nothing in a short window. Fix: drive the periodic log from a wall clock (e.g. `ffi GetTickCount64`/`os.time` every 5 s, or every ~4 presents in-match), and keep the immediate "new anomaly" log (615) as is. The anomaly-triggered path itself is correct (resolution back to `0:0` also logs because the signature changes).
QA conditions (Treant single-player bot game, after reload): (i) after the cadence fix, `[hud] ... overlaps=0 offscreen=0` present within 60 s; (ii) no `[ui] textwidth-fallback` line; (iii) two HUD screenshots 2-3 s apart for marker stability + Del open/close with an outside click not leaking (the R1/R2 QA conditions from 20:26 still apply).

Addendum 20:40 — PM's extra checks for task-13:
- **R11_TextWidthW signature/units**: native `extern "C" float R11_TextWidthW(const char*, int size, int bold)` (render11.cpp:389-393) vs Lua cdef `float R11_TextWidthW(const char*, int, int);` (overlay.lua:25-26) — returns **pixels as float**, same units as the RCmd x/y space; both sides agree. It measures the same `adv*scale` that `Text()` advances, so "exact by construction" holds for the drawn width (visual softness at <28 px stays the parked task-3 atlas item).
- **No per-frame cost regression**: `hudFromFrame`/`hudDump`/`hudLog` run inside `overlay.present`, which is called once per bot tick (loader.tick), not per rendered frame; cost = one `R11_TextWidthW` per text command + O(n²) over text boxes only (HUD ~10-60 texts → ≤~1800 comparisons), i.e. negligible at tick cadence. No new allocation in the native per-frame path (Lua publishes a bounded RCmd list as before).
- **Overlap guard cannot spam / mis-measure**: anomaly logging is transition-driven (`sig ~= hudLastSig`, overlay.lua:613-616) → one line per new anomaly state and one on resolution; periodic line is current-count-driven (see required cadence fix above). Measurement uses exact native widths + `h=ceil(size*1.2)` matching Bahnschrift ink height, and excludes the intentional 1px shadow pair, so it will not fire on hud.lua's normal shadow/main pairs (verified against hud.lua:37-38 geometry).

This is the **third stale-RVA instance** (host16 PUO, native fallback, Lua direct path) and explains the post-horn silent exits.
**Gate of the proposed change (file `orders.lua` 36,778 B @01:28:58 md5 `2603357B4D90A0D278D9BC738C792A66`): REJECT — blocking logic bug.** Verified good: RVA now `0x1B5F9B0` (:57); `M.init()` reads first 4 bytes and logs `[order] PUO rva=0x%X sig=ok|MISMATCH` (:295-299); returns init error on mismatch (:300-303); `luajit loadfile` **OK**; only stale-RVA references left are a stale comment (:16) and the host16 audit line. **BLOCKING:** `PUO` is assigned **before** the signature check (`:294`) and is **not cleared** on mismatch; `M.init()` starts with `if PUO then return true end` (:289) and `M.prepare()` only calls init when `not PUO` (:589-592). 1. assign the cast to a local candidate, **set `PUO` only after the signature check passes**;
2. on mismatch set `PUO = nil`, keep a **sticky `initErr`**, and make `M.init()` begin `if initErr then return nil, initErr end` before the `if PUO` fast-path;

4. strengthen the guard to the 8-byte historical prologue `4C 89 4C 24 20 44 89 44` (matches host16’s 25-byte sig prefix) — 4 bytes is weaker than the “never call a wrong address” claim;
5. fix the stale comment at :16.

Preserve/record a true pre-change copy if one exists; otherwise record old hash unknown and keep the new file’s provenance in md5.txt.
**Acceptance evidence for the fixed file:** `luajit loadfile` OK; `[order] PUO rva=0x1B5F9B0 sig=ok` **and** host16 `PUO resolved signature … rva=0x1b5f9b0 sig=ok matches=1` in the same host.log (both paths agree); no `sig=MISMATCH`; order pipeline behaves (orders reach engine, hero last-hits/XP); frozen/provenance record updated (no separate manifest file found under `D:\hake\lotus` — record in `host16.dll.md5.txt`; if a manifest exists elsewhere, point to it).

### 2026-11-17 23:10 — ROOT CAUSE CONFIRMED (hook inside `gameoverlayrenderer64`) — **v4h architecture approved with hard guards; v4i and v4h both REJECT as built**

Lead's dump evidence is decisive and consistent with all observations: `[20:03:37.490] host: Present hook installed at 00007FF85CCFC130`; `dota2.exe.25052.dmp` maps that address **inside `gameoverlayrenderer64` (`0x7ff85cc60000–0x5ce33000`, dxgi is `0x7ff89d900000+`, host16 `0x7ff80c690000+`)**; raw bytes at the target `e9 cb 3e e4 ff 90 90 90 90 90` = our rel32 jmp + nops. Mechanism: Steam replaces the swapchain vtable's `Present` slot with its own handler; we read `vtable[8]` and inline-patch **Steam's renderer code**, corrupting it; at the horn presentation switch the NV driver fail-fasts. Without Steam we patch `dxgi!Present` and survive; plain Dota + Steam survives (one patcher); every build including 422751ED crashes; mask-0 zero-draw crashes; H2c irrelevant. **Root cause accepted; two-patcher framing superseded.**
**(a) v4h must be vtable-only in-match — CONFIRMED.** Temporary inline patch is acceptable **only** to capture the swapchain, restored immediately inside that same call before forwarding (chaining to the captured old slot = Steam handler as `g_origPresent`). Factory-vtable capture is the preferred product long-term but is not available from our injection point without another code patch, so temp-patch is approved for the diagnostic window **with the guards below**.
**(b) Evidence lines: necessary but not sufficient as implemented.** Required additionally: `hook target=<module>+<off>` (module-resolved, e.g. `gameoverlayrenderer64+0x…`), `hook old=<module>+<off>` (vtable[8] before swap), **full saved bytes hex + read-back equality** in `inline patch restored ok=1 bytes=…`, `vtable present patched sc=… slot=8 old=… new=…`, and `vtable copy entries=<n>`; plus a **patch-absence re-verification line ~30 s into the match** (target bytes still equal saved pre-patch bytes). Existing `inline patch restored ok=%d … (7 bytes)` is not enough.
**(c) v4i still REQUIRED first** (hook-presence vs DLL/process-presence control); if it crashes there is an additional unknown to resolve before trusting v4h. **But v4i as built is REJECT: `UiThread` unconditionally installs `SetWindowsHookExA(WH_MOUSE_LL/WH_KEYBOARD_LL)` (`host.cpp:601-604`) — provenance claims they are skipped with a log under `R11_NO_HOOKS=1`; the code does not gate them.** Fix: skip + log `UiThread hooks disabled by env R11_NO_HOOKS=1`, or restate the v4i contract (we require zero hooks).
**(d) Required guards (v4h):**
1. **Vtable copy must not be truncated: `malloc(32*8)`/`memcpy(…,32*8)` (`host.cpp:261-263`) leaves slots ≥32 out of bounds** — IDXGISwapChain3 needs ≥40 (GetCurrentBackBufferIndex 36, CheckColorSpaceSupport 37, SetColorSpace1 38, ResizeBuffers1 39, SetHDRMetaData 40). **BLOCKING.** Copy ≥48 entries (readability-checked via VirtualQuery; log count) and preserve all entries.
2. **Restore the instance vtable pointer on shutdown/unload** (`R11_UnhookAll()` export + call from host shutdown) — currently no restore path; freed/absent copy on unload = crash class.
3. **Failure paths must restore the inline capture patch** (`host.cpp:262/266` return without `RestoreInlinePatch()`), with log `vtable hook failed - inline patch restored, no hooks in match`.
4. **Resolve and log the capture target's module+offset; refuse unknown modules** — if target ∉ {dxgi, gameoverlayrenderer64}, log `hook target unknown module - capture skipped` and do not patch (no overlay draw, no crash).
5. Log `hook target`/`hook old` module+offset, full saved-byte hex, vtable copy count, and patch-absence re-verification (see (b)).
6. Install on the captured render thread only (log tid; if not `g_presentTid`, skip).
7. No inline hooks in-match: resize inline installs already skipped (`:491`); keep `R11_INLINE_HOOK` unset; also ensure no fallback leaves the capture patch alive.

### 2026-11-17 21:30 — MAJOR NARROWING: drawing EXONERATED; Present **inline-hook** now prime suspect; v4i control + v4h vtable-hook architecture ruled

v4g mask-0 run (hook-only, **zero D3D calls**): init green (`decoder selftest ok=1 rbLen=10 rb1Len=15 negRejected=1`, `probe getDescSlot=12 resizeSlot=13`, `hook ResizeBuffers slot=13 addr=…` no mismatch, ResizeBuffers1 disabled, MT now=1), stage trace `mask=0 stage=hookonly → present` every frame with `hud=0 est=0 verts=0 cap=0 drew=0`; horn `gs=150.900 state 4→5` then crash 2 s later with identical `nvwgf2umx 0xC0000409/0xE4BCDD`. **No `resize begin` line ever appeared** → engine does not resize at the horn ⇒ **H2c (retained refs/resize) disproven as the trigger** (v4g hygiene remains correctness, not the fix). Drawing is not implicated (mask 0 = zero D3D calls and it still crashes). Prime suspect: **two overlays both inline-patching `dxgi!Present`** (ours + Steam `gameoverlayrenderer64`); at the horn presentation switch the stacked prologue/chain goes inconsistent and the driver fail-fasts. Consistent with plain Dota + Steam overlay surviving (single patcher).
**(a) Product architecture ruling: VTABLE-HOOK IS THE RIGHT DIRECTION** (no inline patch of Present). Confirmed advantages: the only live patcher is Steam's; our handler is entered through the copied vtable slot and forwards to `dxgi!Present` (Steam-patched entry → Steam hook → real Present), so the chain is the same single-patch chain that plain Dota survives. Implementation requirements: (i) capture swapchain once from the temporary inline hook, **save the exact bytes before patching, restore them immediately** (restoring the pre-patch bytes puts Steam's E9 back intact — do this while inside the first Present so no concurrent Present); (ii) copy the vtable (read its size to the highest slot we replace + margin), replace slot 8, install instance vtable pointer (VirtualProtect on the instance/heap page), and restore the original vtable pointer on shutdown; (iii) **no inline hooks at all during the match** — drop the ResizeBuffers/ResizeTarget/SetFullscreenState hooks in v4h (H2c disproven; they are unnecessary and would re-introduce code patching); keep them only in a diagnostic build if needed; (iv) forward with the exact args and return the original HRESULT; (v) document/limit swapchain recreation: Dota creates the swapchain once and the evidence shows no resize at the horn, so a single-instance vtable swap is sufficient for the product path — add a factory/`CreateSwapChain*` vtable hook later only if real-world evidence shows recreation; (vi) do not touch Steam's code or bytes.
**(b) v4i control: REQUIRED, run before v4h.** `R11_NO_HOOKS=1` (DLL + Lua loaded, **zero hooks**: no inline, no vtable, no WndProc/input hooks if feasible — at minimum no DXGI hooks), 2+ horns + 15-min soak. It separates DLL/Lua/thread presence from hook presence: crash ⇒ the crash is not caused by our DXGI hooking (then even a vtable hook is unlikely to fix; escalate DLL-presence/loader/thread side); survives ⇒ hook presence implicated and v4h is the decisive test. Cheap; the A0/A1b/A1c matrix is moot and is formally retired.
**(c) Required evidence:**
- **v4i**: log line proving no-hooks mode (`hooks disabled by env` / no `hook … installed` / no `[r11] present hr=` / no stage lines), Lua loader/host lines proving DLL+Lua loaded; 2+ horns + 15-min soak; crash/no-crash, dump if crash; module list (`R11_LogModules` if it still works without hooks — if hookless mode disables it, log from host via loader); `gameoverlayrenderer64` presence.
- **v4h**: `vtable present patched sc=… slot=8 old=… new=…`; `inline patch restored ok` + read-back verification that the Present prologue equals the pre-patch saved bytes (Steam's E9 intact); no `Present prologue mismatch`; stage trace still produced (`mask/… drew=1 mapOk=1` with normal mask 7); `present hr=…` logs; `R11_LogModules`; 2+ horns + 15-min soak with **Steam overlay ENABLED**; overlay HUD/menu visible and Steam overlay still functional; crash status + symbolized dump if it crashes. If v4h crashes with the same signature, the two-patcher theory is insufficient → next escalation: no DXGI hooks in-match (draw only in lobby via input-thread-free path), or hook the DXGI factory vtable instead, with a symbolized dump.
**Gate: REJECT-open; A0/A1b/A1c retired; H2c marked disproven-as-trigger; mask-0 is the new baseline control.**

### 2026-11-17 19:55 — v4d (H2c) **REJECT: blocking vtable-index defects + residual backbuffer ref**; do NOT activate

Verified hashes/artifacts: `render11.cpp` 54,297 md5 `EA6E30E337C4CF1A7218748B95D11720`, `host.cpp` 27,747 md5 `305CDAFF5EB3ADC0EBD1418461AA2F7B`, `host16_new_v4d.dll` 775,680 md5 `A079B350A00ABC5408BD23BEB7B56127`, PDB 8,081,408 + MAP 1,201,241 retained; provenance present. H2c items verified as claimed: `g_rtv` no longer created anywhere in InitD3D (only GetBuffer→GetDesc→Release), cached RTV default 0 (:68), `R11_ReleaseBackbufferRefs()` releases cache/uncached/g_rtv, hooks log begin/hr, handlers release-before-forward and always forward, generic inline hook tolerant (logged skip), `R11_LogModules` auto first Present, v4c diagnostics retained, ABI unchanged.
**BLOCKING defects:**
1. **Wrong vtable indices.** `host.cpp:323` uses `vt[13]` for `ResizeBuffers` and `host.cpp:215` uses `vt3[31]` for `ResizeBuffers1`. Correct absolute vtable indices for IDXGISwapChain/IDXGISwapChain3 (IUnknown 0-2 + IDXGIObject 3-6 + IDXGIDeviceSubObject 7): **Present=8, GetBuffer=9, GetDesc=10, ResizeBuffers=11, ResizeTarget=12, GetContainingOutput=13**; SC1 16-26, SC2 27-33, **SC3: GetCurrentBackBufferIndex=34, CheckColorSpaceSupport=35, SetColorSpace1=36, ResizeBuffers1=37, SetHDRMetaData=38** (developer's claimed 13/31 are off by 2/6). As built, the hooks would attach to `GetContainingOutput` and `GetFrameLatencyWaitableObject` with mismatched signatures → original called with wrong args (**likely crash / undefined behavior**). **Do NOT activate v4d as built.** Fix: use slot 11 and QI-IDXGISwapChain3 slot 37; log resolved slot+addr at install; keep prologue-skip path.
2. **Residual backbuffer ref across frames.** With `R11_CACHED_RTV=0` (the new default), `CacheRtv` stores the frame's RTV in `g_uncachedRtv` (:127) and only releases it at the **next** frame's CacheRtv call (:123) or on ReleaseBackbufferRefs — so **one backbuffer RTV/ref is still retained across the frame boundary**, which is exactly the H2c hazard (ResizeBuffers called between frames while our last RTV is alive). Fix: release `g_uncachedRtv` at the **end of R11_Render** (after state restore) each frame; keep the ResizeBuffers hook as belt-and-braces. Then H2c item 1 ("no refs across frames") is actually true.
3. **Thread-safety/lock note required:** `R11_ReleaseBackbufferRefs` may run from the engine's ResizeBuffers thread while the Present thread touches the same cache. In practice Dota renders/presents/resizes on the D3D11RenderThread (same tid); record `g_presentTid` and in ReleaseBackbufferRefs log+skip if `GetCurrentThreadId() != g_presentTid` (or add a small lock). State the assumption in code/evidence and confirm same-tid in the run logs.
**A0-prerequisite question:** A0-on-v4c is **not a hard prerequisite** — after these fixes, v4d-first is acceptable as the product-candidate test (it directly tests H2c); keep v4c A0/A1b/A1c queued as the fallback matrix if v4d crashes. Required v4d run evidence: `R11_LogModules` line, `resize begin/hr` lines if a resize occurs at horn, stage trace with tid, 2+ horns, no crash/15-min soak, plus PDB saved if it crashes.
**Gate: REJECT-open; v4d NOT cleared.** Fix items 1-3 → rebuild new hash + `cl /c` + provenance + PDB/MAP → fast re-gate.

### 2026-11-17 20:20 — v4 design refinement: cheapest-decisive A/B order, stage-trace spec, gate criteria (+H0 multithread suspect)

Refined co-design for the diagnostic v4 (build offline; no swap until Lead):

Prime-suspect diagnosis accepted: the old vertex budget `(hudN+menuN)*64+4096` could under-estimate (text 6 verts/glyph; rounded-rect up to 786; circle outline 576), `PushVert`/`memcpy` could then run past the mapped dynamic VB `ByteWidth` into driver-owned memory — consistent with `0xC0000409` fail-fast inside `nvwgf2umx` with host16.dll absent from the faulting-module list, timing seconds-to-minutes after Del/menu interaction. Guard patch addresses the class correctly.

**(a) Budget coverage — PASS, every dispatch branch is covered with exact/over estimates.** `CmdVerts` (:406-419) vs `DrawCmd`/`DrawWorldCmd`:
- kinds 0/6 text: `TextVerts` (:393-405) = 6 + 6/codepoint counted to NUL, **over-counts** every codepoint that `Text()` (:278-297) skips (cp<32, cp≥0x500, `adv<=0`, truncated UTF-8) → estimate ≥ actual ✓.
- kind 1 SolidRect = 6 ✓ exact; kind 2 Line = 6 ✓ exact (early-out ≤6); kind 3 outline = `seg*6` (:410) with the same seg formula as `CircleOutline` (:365-376) ✓ exact; kind 4 filled = `seg*3` matching `FilledCircle` fan (:353-363) ✓ exact; kind 5 rounded = `18 + seg*12` = 3 SolidRects + 4 same-r FilledCircles (:378-390) ✓ exact, incl. the `r<=0.01` 6-vert SolidRect path; kind 7 world line = 6 (Line, ≤6) ✓; kind 8 world filled = `seg*3` matching `FilledCircle` ✓; kind 9 world outline = `seg*6` matching `CircleOutline` ✓; clip kinds 10/11 (if published) → `CmdVerts` default 0 and `DrawWorldCmd` ignores them → consistent (note: clip cmds are currently *ignored*, a known W4 gap, unrelated to this crash). Extra `estVerts=64` + `EnsureCap(estVerts+256)` (:657-660) covers the post-command hero→goal marker Line (6 verts, :672-697). No under-estimated branch found.
**(b) `need > g_vbBytes` guard (:725-731) — correct as last-resort; move the check before `Map` for hygiene.** With `EnsureCap` only growing `g_cap` and `PushVert` clamping `g_n≤g_cap`, and `g_vbBytes = g_cap*sizeof(Vert)`, the guard is normally unreachable defense-in-depth — good. Two hardening items: (i) **post-`EnsureCap` re-check**: the `!g_buf||!g_vb` resource-null test (:647) runs *before* `EnsureCap` (:660); if `CreateBuffer` fails inside `EnsureCap` (:248-252) it nulls `g_vb` and the frame then calls `Map(NULL)` → invalid-call/risk. Add `if (!g_buf || !g_vb) { log-once; restore state; return; }` after `EnsureCap`. (ii) optional: pre-compute `need` and skip the `Map` when `need > g_vbBytes` (avoids a pointless map/unmap) — no correctness impact.
**(c) Residual unbounded writes — none found in the vertex path** (all geometry funnels through the clamped `PushVert` :255-270; atlas `memset`/loops are `ATLAS*ATLAS`-bounded with `y+gh<ATLAS` placement :125-126; VB memcpy bounded :729; draw count made multiple of 3 :734-737; menu/hud counts clamped at publish :464/:467 and re-validated :640). Two **read-side/robustness** residuals worth fixing in the same drop: (i) `RCmd.text` is a fixed `char[64]` — guarantee NUL termination in `R11_PublishHud/Menu` (`text[63]=0` per copied cmd) so `Text/TextVerts` can never walk past the field; (ii) `UpdateViewMatrix` (:318-325) memcpys the view-matrix constant at `client.dll+0x6221CB0` with **no** `RPtrOk` guard (pre-existing, live-proven, but a wrong offset would be a read AV — cheap to wrap).
**Verdict: PASS(code) conditional on (i) post-EnsureCap null re-check + (ii) text NUL enforcement (and optional pre-Map vb-size check); then Lead schedules rebuild.** Native gate HELD until post-rebuild live evidence: menu-open Del stress (the historical repro: open/draw menu, spam Del > minutes) with **no** `skip vert overflow` / `skip draw reason=vb-size` lines, no crash, and `frame … verts=… cap=…` lines showing cap ≥ max verts; plus a normal-match soak with no fail-fast. No rebuild/relaunch by me.

### 2026-11-16 21:02 — task-17 (P0-perf: entity.lua per-tick snapshot caching) — owner `developer` — **PASS with QA evidence conditions**

Files read: `entity.lua` 20:32:08 (cache layer 58-206, getLocalHero 315-347, buildHandleMap 252+), `bot_loop.lua` 20:31:05 (`pcall(entity.beginTick)` at :44), `dev_entity_check.lua` 20:31:47. Loadfile 3/3 OK (re-run by me); offline suite re-run by me: `dev_entity_check: 24 checks, 0 failed`.

Verified in code:
- **Snapshot lifecycle is coherent**: `M.beginTick()` (83-89) sets `tickDriven=true`, resets `tickBuilds`, forces `listAt=0`/`handleMapGen=-1`; the first `M.iter()` in the tick builds the identity array with ONE list walk (148-168, bumps `listGen`, invalidates the handle-map gen, increments `tickBuilds`, logs `[entity] iter build ms=.. n=.. tickBuilds=..`); later iterators return the snapshot (201-204); `byHandle` map is built once per snapshot gen (252-257). So `tickBuilds=1` (iter) → `2` (handle map) is the expected live pattern, and the counters are increment sites I can see (not merely claimed).
- **Invalidation ordering is safe**: `M.invalidate()` (92-97) sets `listAt=0`, bumps `listGen`, drops the handle map and name cache; the next build re-anchors the head (`headAt=0`/`M.init()` path at 175-177) — an invalidated cache can never serve a pre-invalidation snapshot, and gen-keyed caches (local hero) are invalidated by the bump.
- **`getLocalHero` cache-hit guard is correct** (315-322): cache is used only when `cachingEnabled()` **and** `localHeroId` is set **and** `localHeroGen == listGen` **and** `listAt ~= 0`; the hit re-validates `designerName` is still a hero and `instance` resolves, otherwise clears and rescans; assigned-hero change triggers `M.invalidate()` before refreshing (340-341); `heroRecord` re-reads live health/team per call (matches the owner's 670→800 assertion). No stale-identity leak across matches found.
- **Semantics preserved**: iteration order is the CEntityIdentity walk order copied verbatim into the array; `byHandle` miss/sentinel returns nil (0/0xFFFFFFFF guard kept, `h % 0x8000` math unchanged); legacy live-walk path is restorable via `_G.ENTITY_NO_CACHE` and the no-driver fallback keeps the 50 ms TTL (58-66) so other `run.txt` drivers behave as before; `M.field` schema-offset caching is additive only.
- `bot_loop.lua:44` change is a single `pcall(entity.beginTick)` per loop iteration (safe if entity is the old module: pcall swallows; and no other behavior touched). Instrumentation is bounded (first 3 builds/tick + every 25th, lines 167-168) and silenceable → cannot flood; `M.stats` exposed for `[perf]`.
Residual risks (accepted, evidence-gated): (a) **mid-tick staleness is the point** — anything that must see entities spawned/despawned *within* a tick now sees tick-start state; only `bot_loop` calls `beginTick` today, so a driver that calls `loader.tick` without it falls back to the 50 ms TTL (documented) — that is not worse than the old behavior but should be stated in the owner's report; (b) `tickBuilds` truthfulness for the byHandle stage is asserted by the 24-check suite, not re-derived by me; (c) the byHandle legacy path under `_G.ENTITY_NO_CACHE` was not exercised by me (owner claims verbatim legacy) — QA can A/B if a handle-resolution regression appears.
QA evidence conditions (Treant bot game, same reload window): `[entity] iter build … tickBuilds=1` then `[entity] byHandle build … tickBuilds=2` per tick with no flood; tick time down vs the pre-task-17 `[perf]` baseline; bot still moves/issues orders (`[order]` lines); local hero identity correct after a match restart (no stale hero); if `[entity]` shows builds >3/tick or `tickBuilds` grows past 2, reopen.
